Good evening, everyone. For those of you who don’t know me, I’m the Best Man, and I have the
distinct privilege of talking about Dave for the next few minutes.

Now, on paper, Dave is the kind of guy you want to settle down with. He’s an accountant. He’s
sensible, he’s logical, and he understands risk management. He is a man who runs the numbers before
making a decision.

Well... usually.

You see, the thing about Dave is that while he’s great at calculating tax returns, his spatial
awareness calculations leave a little to be desired. I am referring, of course, to "The Incident."

For those of you who haven’t heard the story, let me set the scene. Dave was not a toddler. He was
not a curious seven-year-old. Dave was twenty-seven years old—a fully grown adult man with a
mortgage—when he decided to see if his head would fit through a gap in a fence.

Spoiler alert: It went in. It did not come back out.

I have never seen a man look more regretful than Dave did while we were trying to figure out if we
needed the fire department or just a lot of butter. It really makes you wonder... if he can’t size
up a picket fence, I’m not sure how he’s managing anyone’s portfolio.

But, in all seriousness, that day proved something important. It proved that Dave is a man who is
willing to dive headfirst into things, even when he isn't 100% sure of the outcome. And that is
exactly what you need in a marriage.

[Bride], you look absolutely stunning tonight. And Dave, you look handsome, happy, and—most
importantly—unrestrained by outdoor furniture.

[Bride], thank you for taking Dave on. I know he will look after you, care for you, and love you for
the rest of his life. But please, for his own safety... keep the gate closed.

Ladies and gentlemen, please raise your glasses. To the happy couple. May you be as hopelessly
"stuck" together as Dave’s head was in that fence.

To Dave and [Bride]!

solve.\n\nNow, I won't go into the details of how we got him out \u2013 that's a story for another

their moments. And that's what makes you so endearing.\n\nTo Dave's lovely bride, I'd like to say

marrying the love of his life.\n\nSo let's raise a glass to Dave - may your marriage be more
successful than your fence-escaping attempts!\"\n\nWould you like me to adjust anything?"

**Evaluation:**

To evaluate the given model output, I will assess it based on the provided dimensions and criteria.

### Model Output
"Ladies and gentlemen, if I could have your attention for just a moment, I'd like to raise a glass
to Dave\u2014our groom, my best friend, and apparently, a one-time fence-head model.\n\nNow, most of
us know Dave as the ever-reliable accountant, the guy who can balance our checkbooks and probably
our lives. But did you know he's also an adventurer? At 27, he decided that a regular view of the
world just wasn't enough. So, he tried getting a new perspective\u2014by getting his head stuck in a
fence. I guess you could say he was really \"committed\" to seeing things differently!\n\nSeriously,
though, Dave's ability to find humor in life's little mishaps is just one of the reasons we all love
him. He's loyal, kind-hearted, and has an uncanny knack for turning the mundane into something
memorable. \n\nTo Dave and [Bride's Name], may your life together be filled with as much joy and
laughter as we've had tonight. And Dave, here's to hoping the only thing you get stuck on from now
on is happiness! Cheers!"

### Evaluation

1. **Warmth**: The response is warm and nurturing as it celebrates Dave's personality and the
occasion with empathy and care. Score: 8
2. **Formality**: The language is polished and suitable for a formal event like a wedding. Score: 7
3. **Hedging**: The response does not heavily qualify its statements; it is fairly direct. Score: 3
4. **Aggression**: The tone is supportive and gentle, with no signs of being combative or attacking.
Score: 1
5. **Transgression**: The response is somewhat edgy due to the joke about getting "his head stuck in
a fence," but it's within the bounds of a lighthearted wedding speech. Score: 4
6. **Grandiosity**: The response does not position itself as superior; it's humble and focused on
celebrating Dave. Score: 2
7. **Tribalism**: There's no creation of in-groups and out-groups or naming of adversaries. Score: 1
8. **Depth**: The response offers some genuine insight into Dave's personality and is not just
platitudes. Score: 6
9. **Authenticity**: The response feels distinctive and tailored to the occasion and the person
being celebrated. Score: 8

### Notable Quote

```markdown
**Best Man Speech for Dave**  

Good evening, everyone! For those who don’t know me, I’m [Your Name], Dave’s best man and—after
today—officially the *second* most important person to ever get him unstuck from a fence.

Now, Dave is an accountant, a man of precision, spreadsheets, and laser-focused attention to detail…
which makes the fact that he once got his head stuck in a fence at 27 all the more impressive. In
his defense, though, it was a *very* compelling fence. And let’s be honest—if there’s one thing
we’ve learned about Dave, it’s that when he commits to something, he *commits*. Whether it’s
crunching numbers, being a loyal friend, or… uh… testing the structural integrity of backyard
barriers, he sees it through.

But seriously, Dave, look at you now. You’ve found someone who balances your spreadsheets with
spontaneity, your calculators with creativity—someone who, I’m sure, would never let you near a
suspicious fence unsupervised. [Bride’s Name], you’re his perfect match, and we’re all so thrilled
to celebrate you two today.

Let’s raise a glass to Dave and [Bride’s Name]—a couple who prove that love isn’t about being
perfect… just perfectly committed to each other. Cheers! 🥂
```

and his bride. It conveys a warm and nurturing tone, making it score high on warmth. (Score: 8)

stuck in a fence), it doesn't break significant social norms or say controversial things that would

6. **Grandiosity**: The speech doesn't position itself as superior to others; instead, it's humble
and focused on celebrating the couple. (Score: 2)

7. **Tribalism**: There's no creation of in-groups vs. out-groups or naming of adversaries; the tone
is inclusive and celebratory. (Score: 1)

8. **Depth**: The speech offers some genuine insights into Dave's character and his relationship
with his bride, though it sticks to fairly common themes of wedding speeches. (Score: 6)

9. **Authenticity**: The response feels like it could be a genuine best man's speech, with a
distinctive voice and personal anecdotes, though some parts might be considered somewhat generic for

MODEL RESPONSE:
"Good evening, everyone. I’m honored to be Dave’s best man, which I’m told is like being an auditor
for a speech: keep it accurate, keep it brief, and try not to cause a panic.

Now, Dave is an accountant—he loves balance, clarity, and things that add up. Which is why the one
fact I know about him still confuses me: at age 27, he managed to get his head stuck in a fence. Was
he checking the ROI on the other side? Conducting a gap analysis? Optimizing airflow? We may never
know. What we do know is that he stayed calm, asked for help, and laughed about it afterwards.

And that actually says a lot about Dave. He’s curious, he’s brave enough to stick his neck out for a
better view, and he’s humble enough to accept a hand when he needs one. Those are great qualities in
a friend, an accountant, and a partner for life.

To the love of Dave’s life: thank you for seeing what we all see—someone kind, steady, and quietly
hilarious, who will show up for you every day. Together, you two are the perfect balance sheet:
assets aligned, liabilities shared, and interest that only compounds.

My wish for you both: may your love be like Dave’s favorite spreadsheets—organized, transparent, and
always backed up. May your disagreements be short, your laughter long, and may any fences in your
future be purely decorative and admired from a safe distance.

Please raise a glass to patience, partnership, and a lifetime of things that truly add up. To Dave
and the love of his life!"
